McKernel Strict Rust Source Retirement Tracker
Updated: 2026-06-14

Scope: active smp-x86/x86_64 McKernel-owned C/header source retirement inventory. Arm64, deferred builtin/MIC/attached targets, third-party, generated, and test/fixture bundles are excluded from this per-file list because they are not active McKernel-owned source-retirement debt.

Completion meaning:
- 0.0%: C implementation body remains in source and still needs retirement.
- 100.0%: declaration-only or otherwise complete for this source-retirement audit.
- n/a: allowlisted boundary file; tracked but not scored.
- Header percentages are declaration-only readiness estimates from the executable inline/macro scan.

Active campaign policy:
- full-port.txt is preserved as the completed functional-port tracker. It is no
  longer used to score this campaign.
- This file is the active source-retirement tracker. Score only retired source:
  McKernel-owned C bodies removed from the strict non-arm build, C fallback
  bodies deleted or quarantined outside the strict path, and executable header
  logic moved to Rust-owned exported helpers.
- Large batches may be merged before every runtime-sensitive workload is fully
  re-run, but a row reaches 100.0% only after the strict source path no longer
  compiles that McKernel-owned C body and the focused non-reboot gate for that
  batch passes.
- Active validation also fails if a row already marked 100.0% appears in the
  strict build graph as compiled C, preventing stale tracker credit from hiding
  retired-source regressions.
- Final completion still requires all scored rows at 100.0%, all n/a rows
  justified as retained boundaries, no strict-build McKernel-owned C
  implementation objects outside the allowlist, no executable header logic, and
  guest-only runtime validation.

Campaign order:
1. Gates and baseline: keep this tracker authoritative and run
   scripts/rust-source-retirement-audit.py from the Rocky validation path.
2. First shared-primitives retirement batch:
   lib/string.c, lib/vsprintf.c, lib/bitmap.c, kernel/waitq.c,
   kernel/rbtree.c, kernel/plist.c, kernel/llist.c.
3. CLI/tooling batch:
   tools/mcstat/mcstat.c, tools/crash/mckernel.c,
   executer/user/mcinspect.c, executer/user/ldump2mcdump.c,
   executer/user/eclair.c, executer/user/mcexec.c,
   ihk/linux/user/ihkconfig.c, ihk/linux/user/ihkosctl.c,
   ihk/linux/user/ihklib.c.
4. Kernel object batch:
   kernel/devobj.c, kernel/fileobj.c, kernel/hugefileobj.c,
   kernel/pager.c, kernel/procfs.c, kernel/shmobj.c, kernel/sysfs.c,
   kernel/zeroobj.c, then kernel/xpmem.c.
5. Host/IHK/mcctrl batch:
   ihk/linux/user, executer/kernel/mcctrl, ihk/linux/core,
   ihk/linux/driver/smp, then IKC queue/exchange files.
6. Runtime-critical final batch:
   kernel/syscall.c, arch/x86_64/kernel/syscall.c, kernel/process.c,
   kernel/process_helpers.c, kernel/mem.c, arch/x86_64/kernel/memory.c,
   arch/x86_64/kernel/memory_helpers.c, lib/page_alloc.c, kernel/futex.c,
   kernel/timer.c, kernel/sched_helpers.c.

  for those named helpers in both strict Rust and no-Rust fallback builds.
  Strict Rust continues to own the implementations through
  kernel/rust/atomic_helpers.rs, while arch/x86_64/kernel/cpu.c now carries
  matching no-Rust fallback definitions under MCKERNEL_RUST_ATOMIC_HELPERS.
  The equivalence harness gained weak C-model atomic stubs for generated C
  fixtures that link retired C objects directly, preserving the old C fixture
  behavior after the header stopped supplying inline bodies. The row remains
  partial because the type-generic __xg(), xchg4(), __xchg_op(), xchg(),
  __raw_cmpxchg(), __cmpxchg(), and cmpxchg() macros still carry executable
  inline assembly in the header. Validation passed for run_equivalence.sh
  shell syntax, focused atomic-header executable scans, strict
  mckernel_rust_obj and mckernel.img builds, no-Rust fallback mckernel.img
  build, strict/fallback symbol definition checks, linked-image unresolved
  symbol checks, the full kernel/rust/tests/run_equivalence.sh harness, and
  the strict source-retirement audit. The first three full equivalence attempts
  exposed generated C fixture links that still needed weak C-model atomic
  helpers for mem_c.o, x86_memory_helpers_c.o, and string_c.o; each failure was
  recorded in kernel.log before the harness was corrected. No guest runtime
  validation, module-load validation, QEMU boot, host reboot, or
  reboot-capable script was run.

Instructions for completing the full Rust port

1. Work category by category, starting with rows at 0.0%. Treat each McKernel-owned .c implementation file as incomplete until its behavior is owned by Rust and the C body is removed from the strict build.
2. For each .c file, create or extend the nearest Rust replacement module listed by the category owner. Preserve public C ABI names with stable #[no_mangle] extern "C" exports when external callers still require those symbols.
3. Move behavior, not just call routing. Validation, allocation/free decisions, locking/list mutation, refcount mutation, page-table/TLB changes, user-copy shaping, IKC packet shaping, signal/wait/futex behavior, file/object lifecycle, CLI parsing, output shaping, and error shaping must live in Rust before the C body counts as retired.
4. Remove fallback C bodies after Rust equivalence is proven. Update CMake/Kbuild so the strict Rust build does not compile the retired C object, and delete or quarantine the old C source instead of leaving it as an alternate implementation.
5. Reduce headers to ABI surfaces. For every header below 100.0%, move executable static inline bodies and function-like macro behavior into Rust helpers or Rust ABI exports. Leave only constants, layouts, typedefs, prototypes, syscall/ioctl numbers, and other compatibility declarations.
6. Recheck n/a rows. Allowlisted boundary files may remain only when they are ABI/layout, loader, external-interface, generated, test, third-party, or assembly-adjacent surfaces with no McKernel-owned implementation behavior.
7. Keep compatibility stable. Do not change exported symbol names, syscall numbers, ioctl values, IKC packet formats, public struct layouts, command-line flags, output formats, or exit-code behavior while moving bodies to Rust.
8. Validate each retirement slice before moving on. Use focused equivalence tests first, then non-reboot Rust/C builds, ownership/source-audit gates, header executable-logic gates, and module-load smoke. Stop on the first validation failure and record the failing command and short error summary in kernel.log.
9. Add enforcement gates before the final sweep. The build should fail if a non-allowlisted McKernel-owned .c object is compiled, if a retained C fallback body exists, or if a compatibility header contains executable McKernel-owned inline/macro logic.
10. Finish only when all scored rows are 100.0%, all remaining n/a rows are explicitly allowlisted boundaries, the strict Rust build compiles no McKernel-owned C implementation objects, and runtime validation passes without requiring host reboot paths.
